Certifying entanglement typically relies on assumptions about measurement devices, which may be unreliable or untrusted. A method that avoids prior device characterization would provide stronger, more robust entanglement verification.

### Method

The authors develop and experimentally implement a gauge-independent entanglement certification protocol using noncontextuality inequalities, likely in a photonic setup. The inequalities are used to witness entanglement without detailed knowledge of the measurement apparatus.

### Conclusion

### TL;DR

### Motivation

Network nonlocality has attracted significant interest, but it is often unclear whether observed nonclassicality is genuinely novel or merely reducible to standard Bell nonlocality.

### Method

The authors define a testable criterion for being outside the shadow of Bell's theorem, called minimal network nonclassicality, and apply it to the 3-chain/bilocality scenario.

### Result

They provide examples of minimally network nonclassical correlations in quantum theory and exotic operational probabilistic theories, proving certain correlations escape Bell's shadow.

### Conclusion

This approach offers a sharper way to assess the novelty of network-nonclassicality and distinguishes it from prior approaches.

## 3. Quantifying Mixed-state Entanglement Via Partial Transpose And Realignment Moments

- **Score**: 8.8 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Quantum  |  **Feed**: Quantum
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-08-19
- **Authors**: Poetri Sonya Tarabunga And Tobias Haug
- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-08-19-2194/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-08-19-2194/)
- **Tags**: `noise`  `entanglement`  `mixed-state entanglement`  `entanglement witnesses`  `partial transpose`

### TL;DR

The paper introduces quantitative entanglement witnesses based on partial transpose and realignment moments for mixed quantum many-body states, with efficient measurement schemes and applications to entanglement detection, Schmidt rank certification, circuit depth, and pseudoentanglement.

### Motivation

Quantifying entanglement in mixed quantum many-body systems is notoriously difficult, and existing tools are largely limited to pure states or require impractical resources.

### Method

The authors construct families of entanglement witnesses from partial transpose and realignment moments, which yield rigorous bounds on entanglement monotones and dimensionality and can be measured via SWAP tests or Bell measurement variants.

### Result

They obtain efficient algorithms to test low/high entanglement for bounded-entropy mixed states, certify Schmidt rank with two-copy measurements and operator Schmidt rank with four-copy measurements, robustly certify quantum circuit depth under noise, and determine the entanglement phase diagram of Haar random states via partial transpose negativity.

### Conclusion

These witnesses enable practical quantification of entanglement in large noisy quantum systems and establish rigorous limits on pseudoentanglement and pseudorandom density matrices, opening new avenues for mixed-state entanglement characterization.

*Keywords: mixed-state entanglement, entanglement witnesses, partial transpose, realignment moments, Schmidt rank certification*

### Motivation

### Conclusion

The results clarify distinct universality classes for purification in hybrid quantum systems, accessible in random circuit architectures and weak measurement protocols.

### TL;DR

The paper derives spectral localization bounds to show that strong system-bath coupling can generate a hierarchy of population relaxation timescales in multilevel open quantum systems.

### Motivation

Strongly coupled open quantum systems often exhibit anomalously slow relaxation, but a general mechanism and rigorous bounds for the emergence of relaxation timescale hierarchies are lacking.

### Method

The authors use the reaction-coordinate polaron-transform framework to map the strong-coupling problem onto an effective weakly dissipative model and analyze the resulting Liouvillian spectrum through spectral localization bounds, corroborating with secular and non-secular master equation simulations.

### Result

For the generalized V model, strong system-bath coupling produces a bright–dark structure in the effective coupling operator, with one collective bright mode and progressively dark modes, leading to separated fast and slow dynamical sectors and a hierarchy of population relaxation timescales.

### Conclusion

The work reveals a general mechanism for slow relaxation in strongly coupled open quantum systems and suggests a route to engineer long-lived states via system-environment interactions.

### Motivation

Reliable quantum memory under circuit-level noise needs decoders that process syndrome information continuously and at a rate comparable to its generation, but repeated measurements make the number of potential error locations grow rapidly with code size and time.

It constructs a space-time Tanner graph with mixed-alphabet error variables to preserve multi-qubit fault correlations, uses an adaptive sliding window to capture long error events, and introduces probabilistic error consolidation to mitigate degeneracy and short cycles.

### Result

### Motivation

Classical LDPC codes benefit from large girth, but quantum LDPC codes must satisfy orthogonality constraints that typically reduce girth and structurally limit minimum distance, hindering practical performance.

### TL;DR

The paper studies concatenated symplectic double codes, showing that a full Clifford group on a single codeblock can be achieved with simple physical single-qubit gates plus qubit relabeling and injected fold-transversal gates, with promising circuit-level performance.

### Motivation

Quantum error correcting codes that work well as memories are now well developed, but fault-tolerant logical gate implementations on them remain complex and less studied, motivating the search for codes with simple, low-overhead logical gate sets.

### Method

Building on the symplectic double construction, the authors analyze concatenated versions of these codes and design logical gate protocols using only physical single-qubit operations, qubit relabeling, and injected fold-transversal gates, then perform circuit-level simulations of state preparation and error correction.

### Result

The full Clifford group on a single codeblock is realized through a functionally simple circuit, and circuit-level simulations show competitive performance at near state-of-the-art physical error rates.

### Conclusion

Concatenated symplectic double codes are argued to be strong candidates as the underlying computational code for medium- to large-scale quantum computers.

### TL;DR

This paper compares resource costs of two leading surface code lattice surgery compilation approaches for Hamiltonian simulation, finding that the optimal scheme depends on the simulation algorithm and circuit characteristics.

### Motivation

Fault-tolerant quantum computing resource estimates often assume serialized Clifford+T circuits for surface code lattice surgery, but direct Clifford+T compilation may benefit highly parallel circuits. The authors aim to determine which compilation family is more efficient for Hamiltonian simulation applications.

### Method

The authors analyze resource costs for Hamiltonian simulation of transverse-field Ising models, the Kitaev honeycomb model, and a complex Hamiltonian under time-varying magnetic fields, comparing serialized Clifford gates versus direct Clifford+T to lattice surgery operations, and considering quantum signal processing versus Trotter-Suzuki algorithms.

### Result

They show that the optimal scheme depends on the simulation algorithm: Trotterization benefits by orders of magnitude from direct Clifford+T compilation for these applications, while quantum signal processing may favor serialization.

### Conclusion

Surface code quantum computers should not use a one-size-fits-all compilation scheme; smart compilers should predict the optimal scheme based on logical circuit metrics such as average circuit density, number of logical qubits, and T fraction.

*Keywords: surface code, lattice surgery, quantum error correction, Hamiltonian simulation, resource estimation*

- **Tags**: `quantum simulation`  `nuclear dynamics`  `first quantization`  `quantum signal processing`  `T-count`

### TL;DR

Characterizes resource requirements for quantum simulation of nuclear dynamics using first quantization with the leading-order pionless EFT Hamiltonian, showing polynomial scaling in particle number and logarithmic scaling in basis size.

### Motivation

Real-time simulations of nuclear systems are classically intractable due to exponential resource costs, motivating the use of quantum computers for scalable predictions of scattering cross sections.

### Method

Employs product formulas and quantum signal processing to simulate time evolution under the LO pionless EFT Hamiltonian in first quantization, with detailed resource estimation for T gates and logical qubits.

### Result

Demonstrates polynomial scaling in the number of particles and logarithmic scaling in single-particle basis states, yielding an exponential improvement over prior second-quantization approaches; low-energy nuclear scattering may require tens of millions of T gates and a few hundred logical qubits.

### Conclusion

Simple nuclear reactions could be within reach of early fault-tolerant quantum platforms, though the work is an application of quantum simulation rather than a contribution to quantum information theory or thermodynamics.

*Keywords: quantum simulation, nuclear dynamics, first quantization, quantum signal processing, T-count*

### TL;DR

The paper shows that three approaches to quantum reference frames—perspective-neutral, algebraic, and effective—are equivalent for ideal reference frames with sharp orientations, and examines the frame dependence of uncertainties.

### Motivation

Multiple frameworks for internal quantum reference frames have emerged with different scopes and symmetry implementations, raising the question of how they relate to one another.

### Method

The authors compare the perspective-neutral Hilbert-space approach, the algebraic approach based on states on a kinematical algebra, and the effective quantum phase-space approach, demonstrating equivalence for ideal QRFs and connecting QRF transformations, including via Page-Wootters projection and gauge-fixing.

### Result

The three formalisms are equivalent for ideal QRFs; the perspective-neutral QRF transformations agree semiclassically with the effective approach, and uncertainties/fluctuations are shown to be frame dependent.

### Conclusion

The work unifies several QRF formalisms and paves the way for extending the effective and algebraic approaches to non-ideal QRFs.

*Keywords: quantum reference frames, gauge symmetry, perspective-neutral, Page-Wootters formalism, algebraic quantum theory*

### Motivation

Quantum phase estimation is crucial for ground-state energy estimation in quantum chemistry, but achieving optimal confidence intervals requires preparing discrete prolate spheroidal sequence states, which are difficult to implement efficiently.

### Method

They represent the optimal control state as a matrix product state with bond dimension 4 and prepare it using a sequence of simple three-qubit operations, showing high accuracy for dimensions up to a large power of 2.

### Result

The MPS approximation is highly accurate for all tested dimensions, and when the dimension is a power of 2, phase estimation can be performed with only three control qubits.

### Conclusion

This approach makes optimal-confidence quantum phase estimation feasible on early fault-tolerant quantum computers with a limited number of logical qubits.

### TL;DR

Organic cation intercalation combined with high pressure tunes interlayer coupling in 1T-TaS2, inducing Ising superconductivity with an in-plane upper critical field exceeding twice the Pauli limit.

### Motivation

Low-dimensional transition metal dichalcogenides host correlated electronic phases highly sensitive to interlayer coupling, but the high-pressure behavior of such low-dimensional systems is largely unexplored.

### Method

Cetyltrimethylammonium cations were intercalated into bulk 1T-TaS2 to weaken interlayer coupling and effectively reduce dimensionality, followed by high-pressure transport measurements up to 58.1 GPa.

### Result

Superconductivity emerges alongside complete suppression of the nearly commensurate charge density wave order, follows a dome-shaped evolution with pressure, and shows Ising superconductivity signatures with in-plane upper critical field above twice the Pauli paramagnetic limit.

### Conclusion

The intercalation-plus-pressure strategy offers a powerful route to tune collective electronic states and search for emergent phenomena in low-dimensional materials.

### Result

They find that the critical exponents vary continuously with the power-law parameter, and KPZ behavior is recovered only in a specific limit, showing that rare events break universality.

### Conclusion

Rare events can destroy universal dynamic scaling in surface growth, requiring a broader framework beyond standard universality classes.

## 399. From The Gravitational Compton Amplitude To Black Hole Perturbation Theory

- **Score**: 0.3 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Phys. Rev. Lett.  |  **Feed**: Physical Review Letters
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-09-25
- **Authors**: N. Emil J. Bjerrum-Bohr, Gang Chen, Carl Jordan Eriksen, And Nabha Shah
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/f939-mjhj](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/f939-mjhj)
- **Tags**: `gravitational Compton amplitude`  `post-Minkowskian gravity`  `black hole perturbation theory`  `worldline effective field theory`  `Schwarzschild-Tangherlini background`

### TL;DR

The paper computes the gravitational Compton amplitude up to third post-Minkowskian order using worldline effective field theory and maps it to black hole perturbation theory.

### Motivation

The authors aim to connect modern scattering-amplitude methods with classical black hole perturbation theory, particularly for understanding gravitational wave emission from binary systems.

### Method

They employ a single worldline effective field theory in a Schwarzschild-Tangherlini background, excise infrared divergences associated with the Weinberg phase, and establish an exact map to black hole perturbation results.

### Result

The gravitational Compton amplitude is obtained to third post-Minkowskian order and shown to directly correspond to known black hole perturbation theory quantities after infrared regularization.

### Conclusion

This work provides a precise bridge between amplitude-based and perturbative black hole approaches, offering a new tool for high-order gravitational dynamics and radiation.

*Keywords: gravitational Compton amplitude, post-Minkowskian gravity, black hole perturbation theory, worldline effective field theory, Schwarzschild-Tangherlini background*

## 400. Fully Charm-Tetraquark Production At Hadronic Collisions With Gluon Radiation Effects

- **Score**: 0.3 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Phys. Rev. Lett.  |  **Feed**: Physical Review Letters
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-09-21
- **Authors**: Yefan Wang And Ruilin Zhu
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/zp4k-2t92](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/zp4k-2t92)
- **Tags**: `charm tetraquark`  `NLO QCD`  `hadronic collisions`  `gluon radiation`  `color-singlet operator`

### TL;DR

The paper reports the first complete next-to-leading-order QCD calculation for fully charm-tetraquark production in hadronic collisions, including gluon radiation effects.

### Motivation

Fully charm-tetraquark states are poorly understood, and precise production calculations are needed to interpret experimental searches at hadron colliders.

### Method

The authors perform a next-to-leading-order QCD calculation for processes involving fully charm-tetraquark states, examining quark configurations and deriving the renormalization constant of the color-singlet four charm quark operator.

### Result

They find that the renormalization constant of the color-singlet four charm quark operator is exactly unity at this order, enabling a complete NLO treatment of tetraquark production with gluon radiation.

### Conclusion

The work provides a benchmark for future theoretical and experimental studies of fully charm tetraquarks at hadronic colliders.

*Keywords: charm tetraquark, NLO QCD, hadronic collisions, gluon radiation, color-singlet operator*

## 401. Singularities And The Divergence Of Spherical Harmonic Expansions: Axially Symmetric Planets

- **Score**: 0.2 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Rep. Prog. Phys.  |  **Feed**: Reports on Progress in Physics (ROPP)
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-09-22
- **Authors**: Gerald V Dunne, Ovidiu Costin, Crichton Ogle, Michael Bevis And Robert A Ashcraft
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/aea024](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/aea024)
- **Tags**: `spherical harmonic expansion`  `gravitational potential`  `singularities`  `convergence divergence`  `axisymmetric planets`

### TL;DR

The paper analyzes the convergence and divergence of spherical harmonic expansions for gravitational potentials using complex singularities, with illustrative axisymmetric planet models.

### Motivation

To build physical intuition for rigorous mathematical results on spherical harmonic expansion divergence and to explain numerical instabilities in polyhedral planet models.

### Method

Complex analysis of potential singularities applied to explicit constant-density axisymmetric planets, avoiding direct computation of expansion coefficients.

### Result

Shows that the region of convergence is determined by complex singularities, provides a geometric understanding of divergence, and identifies the origin of numerical instabilities in polyhedral models.

### Conclusion

Offers new methods to determine convergence regions and a geometric perspective on divergence, with key ideas generalizable beyond the axisymmetric constant-density case.

*Keywords: spherical harmonic expansion, gravitational potential, singularities, convergence divergence, axisymmetric planets*
